<!--
Hi everyone! I’m Josh – I’m a full time independent open source maintainer. I focus on static analysis tooling around JavaScript & TypeScript – most notably, typescript-eslint, the tooling that allows you to run ESLint and Prettier on TypeScript code. 
I’m also the author of the recent Learning TypeScript book, published by O’Reilly.
-->

<!--
This is the technical, precise term for TypeScript: that it’s a “superset” of JavaScript. Meaning, it’s a programming language that contains all the syntax of JavaScript, plus new syntax.
-->

<!--
I’ll give you an example of where TypeScript really shines. I present to you an example run function, trimmed down to show just its action and prefix parameter properties. That action prop is meant to be used for a function that’s called between two console logs.
Let’s play one of my favorite programming games: “is this safe?”
At first glance, the code looks like it should be fine. Presumably action is a function that’s always passed in, and prefix is a string. Nothing in this function alone would indicate to us that there are any issues.
But! Without TypeScript’s knowledge we have no way of linting about a plethora of potential pitfalls:
Maybe action and/or prefix aren’t always provided
Maybe action is meant to take in arguments
Maybe it’s expected that we return the result of calling action
Without a description of the code’s intent, we’re left with just the values: which is often not informative enough for day-to-day development.
-->

</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">0.5</span><span style="color:#B5CEA8;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">) &#123;</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">    value</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> = </span><span style="color:#CE9178;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">"Conf"</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">;</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">&#125; </span><span style="color:#C586C0;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">else</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> &#123;</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">    value</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> = </span><span style="color:#B5CEA8;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">1337</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">;</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">    // Type 'number' is not assignable to type 'string'.</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">&#125;</span></p></div>
<div class="pptx-shape" style="border-top:4.5px solid rgb(192,0,0);height:0px;left:214.93px;top:325.21px;width:55.32px"></div>
</div>

<!--
This variable, ‘value’, is given an initial value of type string. That means value is only ever allowed to be a string. If TypeScript sees that we try to provide it a number, it’ll yell at us in the form of a type error.
Note that this code is syntactically valid – so it can be transpiled down to output JavaScript. But TypeScript will log an error when doing so.
But, what if we have a variable whose type can’t be inferred rom the initial value? In that case, we’ll need some way of indicating to TypeScript what the intended type is meant to be.
-->

---
layout: none
transition: { name: fade, duration: 350 }
---

<div class="pptx" style="background-color:rgb(7,14,21)">
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:104.38px;left:66px;top:28.75px;width:828px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="padding-left:-0.2px;font-family:'Regular Bold';font-size:44px;line-height:47.52px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'Regular Bold';font-size:44px">Type Annotations</span></p></div>
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:342.63px;left:163.13px;top:106.63px;width:633.75px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#569CD6;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">let </span><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">value</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">: </span><span style="color:#4EC9B0;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">string</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">;</span></p><p style="font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><br><span style="font-size:18px;text-decoration:line-through">if</span><span style="color:#C586C0;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> (</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">Math</span><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">.</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">random</span><span style="color:#DCDCAA;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">() &gt; </span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">0.5</span><span style="color:#B5CEA8;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">) &#123;</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">    value</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> = </span><span style="color:#CE9178;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">"Conf"</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">;</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">&#125; </span><span style="color:#C586C0;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">else</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> &#123;</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">    value</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> = </span><span style="color:#B5CEA8;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">1337</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">;</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">    // Type 'number' is not assignable to type 'string'.</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">&#125;</span></p></div>
<div class="pptx-shape" style="border-top:4.5px solid rgb(192,0,0);height:0px;left:214.93px;top:325.21px;width:55.32px"></div>
</div>

<!--
Enter type annotations: a way to indicate to TypeScript the type of a construct such as a variable.
Variables that are given a type annotation also stay the same type always. Here we’re saying that value is type string, and so are not allowed to provide a number (or any other not-string typed values).
Keep in mind that type annotations are purely a development-time construct. They’re erased when your TypeScript syntax is transpiled over to JavaScript.
-->

---
layout: none
transition: { name: fade, duration: 350 }
---

<div class="pptx" style="background-color:rgb(7,14,21)">
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:104.38px;left:66px;top:28.75px;width:828px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="padding-left:-0.2px;font-family:'Regular Bold';font-size:44px;line-height:47.52px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'Regular Bold';font-size:44px">Evolving </span><span style="color:#FFFFFF;font-family:'Regular Bold';font-size:44px">Anys</span></p></div>
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:342.63px;left:163.13px;top:106.63px;width:633.75px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#569CD6;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">let </span><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">value</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">; </span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">// Type: any</span></p><p style="font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><br><span style="font-size:18px;text-decoration:line-through">value</span><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> = </span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">"THAT"</span><span style="color:#CE9178;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">; </span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">// Type: string</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">value</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">.</span><span style="color:#DCDCAA;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">toUpperCase</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">(); </span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">// Ok</span></p><p style="font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><br><span style="font-size:18px;text-decoration:line-through">value</span><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px"> = </span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">1337</span><span style="color:#B5CEA8;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">; </span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">// Type: number</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">value</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">.</span><span style="color:#DCDCAA;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">toPrecision</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">(</span><span style="color:#B5CEA8;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">1</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">); </span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">// Ok</span></p><p style="font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><br><span style="font-size:18px;text-decoration:line-through">value</span><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">.</span><span style="color:#D4D4D4;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">toUpperCase</span><span style="color:#DCDCAA;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">();</span></p><p style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px;line-height:19.44px;margin-top:10px"><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">// Error: '</span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">toUpperCase</span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:18px">' does not exist on type 'number'.</span></p></div>
<div class="pptx-shape" style="border-top:4.5px solid rgb(192,0,0);height:0px;left:234.05px;top:374.47px;width:124.82px"></div>
</div>

<!--
If we don’t provide an initial value for our variables, TypeScript will treat it as what’s called an evolving any. Meaning, the variable’s type will start out as any – it can be anything. Which is super unsafe! We don’t like the any type! It allows us to bypass all sorts of type checking.
But, whenever we assign a value to the variable, TypeScript will narrow the variable’s type to that value’s type. It ‘evolves’ the variable’s type as it’s used.
-->

---
layout: none
transition: { name: fade, duration: 350 }
---

<div class="pptx" style="background-color:rgb(7,14,21)">
<div class="pptx-shape pptx-text" style="height:289.38px;left:149.63px;top:143.75px;width:660.75px;justify-content:center;padding-bottom:3.6px;padding-left:7.2px;padding-right:7.2px;padding-top:3.6px"><ul><li style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px;line-height:30.24px;margin-top:10px;color:#D4D4D4;list-style-type:'• ';margin-left:18px"><span style="color:#569CD6;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px"> null</span></li><li style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px;line-height:30.24px;margin-top:10px;color:#D4D4D4;list-style-type:'• ';margin-left:18px"><span style="color:#569CD6;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px"> undefined</span></li><li style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px;line-height:30.24px;margin-top:10px;color:#D4D4D4;list-style-type:'• ';margin-left:18px"><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px"> boolean </span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px">// true or false</span></li><li style="font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px;line-height:30.24px;margin-top:10px;color:#D4D4D4;list-style-type:'• ';margin-left:18px"><span style="color:#9CDCFE;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px"> string  </span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px">// "", "Hi!", "abc123", …</span></li><li number  </span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px">// 0, 2.1, -4, …</span></li><li bigint  </span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px">// 0n, 2n, -4n, …</span></li><li symbol  </span><span style="color:#6A9955;font-family:'Menlo';font-size:28px">// Symbol(), Symbol("hi"), …</span></li></ul></div>

It was the invention of the null reference in 1965… This has led to innumerable errors, vulnerabilities, and system crashes, which have probably caused a billion dollars of pain and damage in the last 40 years.</span></p><p style="font-family:'Regular Bold';font-size:24px;line-height:28.8px;text-align:center"><span style="color:#C8D7FA;font-family:'Regular Bold';font-size:24px">—Tony Hoare, 2009</span></p></div>
